数据/标签稀缺下的跨 暴发 迁移学习:流行病学 的基础 ML 模型

Cross-Outbreak Transfer Learning Under Data/Label Scarcity: Foundation ML model for epidemiology
作者： Vikas Ramachandra
摘要中文翻译摘要背景:早期的 暴发 行列表是高价值但困难的机器学习数据。它们的模式、缺失模式、病例定义和确认过程随着病原体和管辖范围的不同而变化。因此,在 暴发 上预训练的模型可以转移有害的捷径,而不是可重复使用的流行病学结构。方法:我们开发了 OBFM-SafeEnsemble,一个受保护的跨 暴发 学习框架。历史 2022 猴痘 行列表(69,595 条记录)提供了源自我监督和源系数先验;主要目标是实时 2026 年本迪布焦病毒病埃博拉病毒株列表(2,900 条记录);24条记录的汉坦病毒线路列表仅用作管道压力测试。终点为确认状态。直接实验室、确认、结果、确认后护理、来源和标识符字段被排除在外,以减少泄漏。OBFM-Lite 首先评估了屏蔽令牌预训练和持续的无标记适应。然后,OBFM-SafePrior 将新的埃博拉头规范化为源学习代币效应,并仅在验证 AUROC 超过预先指定的增益阈值时才激活传输。OBFM-SafeEnsemble 训练了一个小型的仅疾病和源优先头部库,并选择最好的头部、前 N 个平均值或凸混合,始终保留仅疾病后备。结果:源屏蔽令牌预训练将域内 猴痘 Transformer AUROC 从 0.839 提高到 0.851。持续的未标记适应将埃博拉零样本 AUROC 从 0.487 提高到 0.523,但绝对转移仍然较弱。在 64 个标记的埃博拉记录中,SafePrior 将 AUROC 从 0.524 提高到 0.664,将 AUPRC 从 0.660 提高到 0.731。SafeEnsemble 在 32、48、64、96 和 128 个标签上的 AUROC 值分别为 0.662、0.656、0.687、0.682 和 0.667。相对于匹配的仅疾病模型,AUROC 增益分别为 +0.068、+0.082、+0.235、+0.064 和 +0.015;AUPRC 收益分别为+0.042、+0.067、+0.160、+0.074 和+0.033。解释:只有在源知识被视为可选的、经过目标验证的正则化并对低方差候选头进行平均之后,在严重标签稀缺的情况下才能实现积极的跨 暴发 迁移。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
早期的 暴发 行列表是高价值但困难的机器学习数据,因为不同病原体和管辖区的模式、缺失模式、病例定义和确认过程各不相同。
设计与对象
历史 2022 猴痘 行列表(69,595 条记录)提供了源自我监督和源系数先验;主要目标是实时 2026 年本迪布焦病毒病埃博拉病毒株列表(2,900 条记录);24条记录的汉坦病毒线路列表仅用作管道压力测试。
核心方法
我们开发了 OBFM-SafeEnsemble,这是一个受保护的跨 暴发 学习框架,用于评估屏蔽令牌预训练和持续未标记的适应,排除直接实验室和确认字段以减少泄漏,并训练一个仅包含疾病和源先验头的小型库,并带有仅疾病后备。
主要结果
源屏蔽令牌预训练将 猴痘 Transformer AUROC 从 0.839 改进到 0.851;持续的未标记适应将埃博拉零样本 AUROC 从 0.487 提高到 0.523;SafePrior 在 64 个标记记录中将埃博拉 AUROC 从 0.524 改进到 0.664,将 AUPRC 从 0.660 改进到 0.731;SafeEnsemble 在 32、48、64、96 和 128 个标签上的 AUROC 值分别为 0.662、0.656、0.687、0.682 和 0.667。
解释与创新
只有在源知识被视为可选的、经过目标验证的正则化并对低方差候选头进行平均之后,在严重标签稀缺的情况下才能实现积极的跨 暴发 迁移。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现为开发用于早期 暴发 检测和响应的强大机器学习模型提供了信息,特别是在标记数据稀缺的资源有限的环境中。
局限与证据强度
由于该研究仅具有抽象性质并且使用了小型压力测试数据集,因此证据强度中等;局限性包括依赖历史数据以及需要在现实场景中进一步验证。

Cross-Outbreak Transfer Learning Under Data/Label Scarcity: Foundation ML model for epidemiology

Authors: Vikas Ramachandra
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background: Early outbreak line lists are high-value but difficult machine-learning data. Their schemas, missingness patterns, case definitions, and confirmation processes change across pathogens and jurisdictions. A model pretrained on one outbreak can therefore transfer harmful shortcuts rather than reusable epidemiological structure. Methods: We developed OBFM-SafeEnsemble, a guarded cross-outbreak learning framework. A historical 2022 mpox line list (69,595 records) supplied source self-supervision and source coefficient priors; a live 2026 Bundibugyo virus disease Ebola line list (2,900 records) was the primary target; and a 24-record hantavirus line list was used only as a pipeline stress test. The endpoint was confirmation status. Direct laboratory, confirmation, outcome, post-confirmation care, provenance, and identifier fields were excluded to reduce leakage. OBFM-Lite first evaluated masked-token pretraining and continual unlabeled adaptation. OBFM-SafePrior then regularized a new Ebola head toward source-learned token effects and activated transfer only when validation AUROC exceeded a prespecified gain threshold. OBFM-SafeEnsemble trained a small library of disease-only and source-prior heads and selected either the best head, a top-N average, or a convex blend, always retaining a disease-only fallback. Results: Source masked-token pretraining improved in-domain mpox Transformer AUROC from 0.839 to 0.851. Continual unlabeled adaptation improved Ebola zero-shot AUROC from 0.487 to 0.523, but absolute transfer remained weak. At 64 labeled Ebola records, SafePrior improved AUROC from 0.524 to 0.664 and AUPRC from 0.660 to 0.731. SafeEnsemble achieved AUROC values of 0.662, 0.656, 0.687, 0.682, and 0.667 at 32, 48, 64, 96, and 128 labels, respectively. Relative to matched disease-only models, AUROC gains were +0.068, +0.082, +0.235, +0.064, and +0.015; AUPRC gains were +0.042, +0.067, +0.160, +0.074, and +0.033. Interpretation: Positive cross-outbreak transfer was achievable under severe label scarcity only after source knowledge was treated as optional, target-validated regularization and averaged across low-variance candidate heads.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Early outbreak line lists are high-value but difficult machine-learning data due to varying schemas, missingness patterns, case definitions, and confirmation processes across pathogens and jurisdictions.
Design and population
A historical 2022 mpox line list (69,595 records) supplied source self-supervision and source coefficient priors; a live 2026 Bundibugyo virus disease Ebola line list (2,900 records) was the primary target; and a 24-record hantavirus line list was used only as a pipeline stress test.
Core methods
We developed OBFM-SafeEnsemble, a guarded cross-outbreak learning framework that evaluated masked-token pretraining and continual unlabeled adaptation, excluded direct laboratory and confirmation fields to reduce leakage, and trained a small library of disease-only and source-prior heads with a disease-only fallback.
Main results
Source masked-token pretraining improved mpox Transformer AUROC from 0.839 to 0.851; continual unlabeled adaptation improved Ebola zero-shot AUROC from 0.487 to 0.523; SafePrior improved Ebola AUROC from 0.524 to 0.664 and AUPRC from 0.660 to 0.731 at 64 labeled records; SafeEnsemble achieved AUROC values of 0.662, 0.656, 0.687, 0.682, and 0.667 at 32, 48, 64, 96, and 128 labels, respectively.
Interpretation and novelty
Positive cross-outbreak transfer was achievable under severe label scarcity only after source knowledge was treated as optional, target-validated regularization and averaged across low-variance candidate heads.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform the development of robust machine learning models for early outbreak detection and response, particularly in resource-limited settings with scarce labeled data.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence strength is moderate due to the abstract-only nature of the study and the use of a small stress test dataset; limitations include the reliance on historical data and the need for further validation in real-world scenarios.

指导防御:MPXV 感染期间 ZBP1 激活 2309240

Directing Defense: ZBP1 Activation during MPXV Infection 2309240
作者： Reem Miah, Anoli Karunathilake, Heather Koehler
摘要中文翻译摘要 介绍 猴痘病毒 (MPXV) 仍然是一个全球健康问题,因为最近 暴发 表现出人类 传播 的增加。这凸显了需要更好地了解病毒 宿主 相互作用,以确定新的 抗病毒药物 策略。Z-DNA 结合蛋白-1 (ZBP1) 是一种可识别 Z-核糖核酸 (Z-RNA) 的先天免疫传感器。Z-RNA 是一种新型病原体相关分子模式 (PAMP),在早期病毒复制过程中产生,可以被 ZBP1 感知。ZBP1 的激活可以启动 抗病毒药物 信号通路,从而限制病毒复制并限制疾病进展。然而,ZBP1 是否有助于控制 MPXV 复制和传播仍然是一个重大的知识空白。方法 用 MPXV 感染野生型 (WT) 和 ZBP1 敲除型 (ZBP1-KO) 骨髓源性巨噬细胞 (BMDM),利用单步和多步生长曲线确定病毒复制和传播。通过噬菌斑测定对病毒滴度进行定量,并通过 RT-qPCR 测量病毒基因表达。在体内,WT 和 ZBP1-KO 小鼠通过鼻内感染 MPXV,模仿呼吸途径 传播。在感染后 1、3 和 5 天 (dpi) 收集肺组织,以量化病毒滴度和病毒基因表达。结果 研究结果表明,ZBP1 缺陷会导致 MPXV 感染的单步和多步生长曲线期间 MPXV 复制和传播增加,病毒滴度升高表明这一点。3dpi时,在ZBP1-KO的肺组织中也观察到病毒基因表达增加,表明宿主 ZBP1在MPXV感染期间发挥抗病毒药物的作用。结论 总之,这些结果表明 ZBP1 在 MPXV 感染过程中发挥 抗病毒药物 作用。然而,还需要进一步研究来定义所涉及的 宿主 防御途径,例如 TBK1 和 NF-κB 信号传导。了解 ZBP1 如何限制 MPXV,因为 Z-RNA 是在早期 MPXV 感染期间产生的,有助于解决理解病毒 宿主 相互作用方面的重大差距。资金来源 GR00012379 主题类别 先天 免疫应答 和 宿主 防御:分子机制 (INM)
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
猴痘病毒(MPXV)仍然是一个全球性的健康问题,因为最近的 暴发 表现出人类 传播 的增加,这突出表明需要更好地了解病毒 宿主 相互作用以识别新的 抗病毒药物 策略。
设计与对象
用 MPXV 感染野生型 (WT) 和 ZBP1 敲除 (ZBP1-KO) 骨髓源性巨噬细胞 (BMDM),以使用单步和多步生长曲线确定病毒复制和传播。在体内,WT 和 ZBP1-KO 小鼠通过鼻内感染 MPXV,模仿呼吸途径 传播。
核心方法
通过噬菌斑测定对病毒滴度进行定量,并通过 RT-qPCR 测量病毒基因表达。在感染后 1、3 和 5 天 (dpi) 收集肺组织,以量化病毒滴度和病毒基因表达。
主要结果
研究结果表明,ZBP1 缺陷会导致 MPXV 感染的单步和多步生长曲线期间 MPXV 复制和传播增加,病毒滴度升高表明这一点。感染后 3d,ZBP1-KO 的肺组织中也观察到病毒基因表达增加。
解释与创新
总之,这些结果表明 ZBP1 在 MPXV 感染期间发挥 抗病毒药物 作用。了解 ZBP1 如何限制 MPXV,因为 Z-RNA 是在早期 MPXV 感染期间产生的,有助于解决理解病毒 宿主 相互作用方面的重大差距。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,针对 ZBP1 激活可能是一种针对 MPXV 感染的新型 抗病毒药物 策略,可能为限制病毒复制和疾病进展的治疗方法的开发提供信息。
局限与证据强度
需要进一步研究来定义所涉及的 宿主 防御途径,例如 TBK1 和 NF-κB 信号传导。证据强度中等,因为该研究提供了直接的实验证据,但缺乏具体途径的机制细节。

Directing Defense: ZBP1 Activation during MPXV Infection 2309240

Authors: Reem Miah, Anoli Karunathilake, Heather Koehler
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Introduction Monkeypox virus (MPXV) remains a global health concern due to the recent outbreak that exhibited an increase in human transmission. This highlights the need to better understand the viral host interactions to identify novel antiviral strategies. Z-DNA Binding Protein-1 (ZBP1) is an innate immune sensor that can recognize Z-ribonucleic acid (Z-RNA). Z-RNA is a novel pathogen-associated molecular pattern (PAMP), produced during early viral replication, and can be sensed by ZBP1. Activation of ZBP1 can initiate antiviral signaling pathways that can restrict viral replication and limit disease progression. However, whether ZBP1 contributes to controlling MPXV replication and spread remains a significant gap in knowledge. Methods Wild-type (WT) and ZBP1-knockout (ZBP1-KO) bone-marrow-derived-macrophages (BMDMs) were infected with MPXV to determine the viral replication and spread using single-step and multi-step growth curves. Viral titers were quantified by plaque assay, and viral gene expression was measured by RT-qPCR. In vivo, WT and ZBP1-KO mice were infected intranasally with MPXV, mimicking the respiratory route transmission. Lung tissues were collected at 1, 3, and 5 days post-infection (dpi) to quantify viral titers and viral gene expression. Results Findings reveal that ZBP1 deficiency results in increased MPXV replication and spread during single and multi-step growth curves of MPXV infection, as indicated by an elevated viral titer. Increased viral gene expression was also observed in lung tissues from ZBP1-KO at 3dpi, suggesting an antiviral role for the host ZBP1 during MPXV infection. Conclusion Together, these results demonstrate that ZBP1 plays an antiviral role during MPXV infection. However, further studies are needed to define the host defense pathways involved, such as TBK1 and NF-κB signaling. Understanding how ZBP1 restricts MPXV as Z-RNA is produced during early MPXV infection can help address a significant gap in understanding the viral host interactions. Funding Source GR00012379 Topic Categories Innate Immune Responses and Host Defense: Molecular Mechanisms (INM)
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Monkeypox virus (MPXV) remains a global health concern due to the recent outbreak that exhibited an increase in human transmission, highlighting the need to better understand viral host interactions to identify novel antiviral strategies.
Design and population
Wild-type (WT) and ZBP1-knockout (ZBP1-KO) bone-marrow-derived-macrophages (BMDMs) were infected with MPXV to determine viral replication and spread using single-step and multi-step growth curves. In vivo, WT and ZBP1-KO mice were infected intranasally with MPXV, mimicking the respiratory route transmission.
Core methods
Viral titers were quantified by plaque assay, and viral gene expression was measured by RT-qPCR. Lung tissues were collected at 1, 3, and 5 days post-infection (dpi) to quantify viral titers and viral gene expression.
Main results
Findings reveal that ZBP1 deficiency results in increased MPXV replication and spread during single and multi-step growth curves of MPXV infection, as indicated by an elevated viral titer. Increased viral gene expression was also observed in lung tissues from ZBP1-KO at 3dpi.
Interpretation and novelty
Together, these results demonstrate that ZBP1 plays an antiviral role during MPXV infection. Understanding how ZBP1 restricts MPXV as Z-RNA is produced during early MPXV infection can help address a significant gap in understanding the viral host interactions.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that targeting ZBP1 activation could be a novel antiviral strategy against MPXV infection, potentially informing the development of therapeutics to limit viral replication and disease progression.
Limitations and evidence strength
Further studies are needed to define the host defense pathways involved, such as TBK1 and NF-κB signaling. The evidence strength is moderate, as the study provides direct experimental evidence but lacks mechanistic detail on the specific pathways.

摘要中文翻译กรมควบคุมโรคได้รับรายงานเหตุการณ์โรค และภัยสุขภาพที่สําคัญ ผ่านโปรแกรมการตรวจสอบข่าวการระบาด กรมควบคุมโรค จากเครือข่ายงานสาธารณสุขทั่วประเทศดังนี้ สถานการณ์ในประเทศ 1. โรคฝีดาษวานร (猴痘) 进化枝 I/Ib จํานวน 5 เหตุการณ์ในกรุงเทพมหานครและจังหวัดปทุมธานี 2. การประเมินความเสี่ยงของการระบาดข องโรคติดเชื้อสเตร็ปโตคอคคัสซูอิส第 2569 章ในพื้นที่เขตสุขภาพ 1 2 และ 3 สถานการณ์ต่างประเทศ สาธารณรัฐประชาธิปไตยประชาชนลาวพบผู ้ป่วยโปลิโอจากเชื้อวัคซีนกลายพันธุ์ (VDPV1) รายใหม่

สรปการตรวจสอบขาวการระบาดของโรค/ภยสขภาพในรอบสปดาหท 29 ป พ.ศ. 2569

Authors: ทีมเฝ้าระวังเหตุการณ์ผิดปกติ
Original abstract (English)
กรมควบคุมโรค ได้รับรายงานเหตุการณ์ โรค และภัยสุขภาพที่สําคัญ ผ่านโปรแกรมการตรวจสอบข่าวการระบาด กรมควบคุมโรค จากเครือข่ายงานสาธารณสุขทั่วประเทศ ดังนี้ สถานการณ์ในประเทศ 1. โรคฝีดาษวานร (Mpox) clade I/Ib จํานวน 5 เหตุการณ์ ในกรุงเทพมหานคร และจังหวัดปทุมธานี 2. การประเมินความเสี่ยงของการระบาดของโรคติดเชื้อสเตร็ปโตคอคคัสซูอิส ในประเทศไทย เดือนกรกฎาคม–สิงหาคม 2569 ในพื้นที่เขตสุขภาพ 1 2 และ 3 สถานการณ์ต่างประเทศ สาธารณรัฐประชาธิปไตยประชาชนลาวพบผู้ป่วยโปลิโอจากเชื้อวัคซีนกลายพันธุ์ (VDPV1) รายใหม่

Question and background
The study aimed to monitor and report significant health events and outbreaks in Thailand through the epidemic news surveillance program.
Design and population
The surveillance program collected reports from public health networks nationwide, focusing on domestic and international health events.
Core methods
The assessment involved evaluating the risk of Streptococcus suis infection in Thailand during July-August 2569 in health zones 1, 2, and 3.

使用混合卷积神经网络的皮肤疾病分类

Skin Disease Classification Using Hybrid Convolutional Neural Network
作者： Alfito Putra Fajar Pratama, Hasih Pratiwi, Isnandar Slamet
摘要中文翻译本研究旨在使用混合卷积神经网络(CNN)方法对皮肤疾病进行分类。混合CNN是CNN模型和机器学习方法之间的集成。使用的分类方法包括逻辑回归、随机森林和XGBoost。经过数据增强过程后,使用 ResNet 作为特征提取和逻辑回归作为分类方法时获得了最高性能结果,准确率为 82%,宏观 f1 分数为 80%,在皮肤病检测中表现出高性能。混合CNN与机器学习方法显示出皮肤病图像特征提取的能力并达到最佳性能。https://doi.org/10.3390/s22020587 - Wang,Y.,Pan,Z.,Zheng,J.,Qian,L.,Li,M.:脉冲星候选分类的混合集成方法。摘要 皮肤病有多种原因,包括接触化学物质、阳光、病毒、细菌和其他因素。皮肤病的分类至关重要,因为它们的症状相似,例如红疹、瘙痒和烧灼感。这些疾病的症状相似,很难通过视觉区分。使用的数据集是MSLD v2.0,是6类皮肤病图像数据,分别是猴痘、牛痘、水痘、麻疹、手足口病、健康。本研究使用三种CNN模型,即EfficientNet、DenseNet和ResNet作为特征提取。数据增强也用于增加数据变化。访问本章 税费计算将在结帐时完成 购买仅供个人使用 其他人正在查看类似内容 参考文献 - Srisantyorini,T.,Cahyaningsih,N.F.: Analisis kejadian penyakit kulit pada pemulung di tempat pengolahan sampah terpadu (TPST) kelurahan sumu...
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在使用混合卷积神经网络(CNN)方法对包括猴痘在内的皮肤病进行分类,因为皮肤病的症状相似,导致视觉区分变得困难。
设计与对象
本研究使用MSLD v2.0数据集,其中包含6类皮肤病图像:猴痘、牛痘、水痘、麻疹、手足口病和健康。
核心方法
该研究采用混合 CNN 方法,将用于特征提取的 CNN 模型(EfficientNet、DenseNet 和 ResNet)与用于分类的机器学习方法(逻辑回归、随机森林和 XGBoost)以及数据增强相结合。
主要结果
数据增强后使用 ResNet 进行特征提取和逻辑回归进行分类实现了最高性能,准确率为 82%,宏观 f1 分数为 80%。
解释与创新
具有机器学习的混合CNN在皮肤病图像特征提取方面表现出强大的能力,并在皮肤病检测方面取得了最佳性能。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可以为皮肤病(包括猴痘)的自动诊断工具的开发提供信息,从而有可能改善临床环境中的早期检测和分类。
局限与证据强度
证据强度中等,因为该研究是在类别有限的特定数据集上进行的,并且性能指标虽然很高,但可能无法推广到所有人群或皮肤病类型。

Skin Disease Classification Using Hybrid Convolutional Neural Network

Authors: Alfito Putra Fajar Pratama, Hasih Pratiwi, Isnandar Slamet
Original abstract (English)
This research aims to classify skin diseases using a hybrid Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) method. Hybrid CNN is an integration between the CNN model and machine learning methods. The classification methods used include logistic regression, random forest, and XGBoost. The highest performance results are obtained when using ResNet as a feature extraction and logistic regression as a classification method after going through the data augmentation process with an accuracy of 82% and a macro f1-score of 80%, which showed high performance in skin disease detection. Hybrid CNN with machine learning method shows the ability to feature extraction of skin disease images and achieves optimal performance. https://doi.org/10.3390/s22020587 - Wang, Y., Pan, Z., Zheng, J., Qian, L., Li, M.: A hybrid ensemble method for pulsar candidate classification. Abstract Skin diseases have a variety of causes, including exposure to chemicals, sunlight, viruses, bacteria, and other factors. Classification of skin diseases is essential because of their similar symptoms, such as red rashes, itching, and burning. The similar symptoms of the diseases make it difficult to distinguish them visually. The dataset used is MSLD v2.0, which is skin disease image data with 6 classes, namely monkeypox, cowpox, chickenpox, measles, HFMD, and healthy. This research uses three CNN models, namely EfficientNet, DenseNet, and ResNet, as feature extraction. Data augmentation is also applied to increase data variation. View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to classify skin diseases, including monkeypox, using a hybrid Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) method due to the similar symptoms of skin diseases making visual distinction difficult.
Design and population
This research used the MSLD v2.0 dataset, which contains skin disease images with 6 classes: monkeypox, cowpox, chickenpox, measles, HFMD, and healthy.
Core methods
The study employed a hybrid CNN approach integrating CNN models (EfficientNet, DenseNet, and ResNet) for feature extraction with machine learning methods (logistic regression, random forest, and XGBoost) for classification, along with data augmentation.
Main results
The highest performance was achieved using ResNet for feature extraction and logistic regression for classification after data augmentation, with an accuracy of 82% and a macro f1-score of 80%.
Interpretation and novelty
The hybrid CNN with machine learning demonstrated strong ability in feature extraction of skin disease images and achieved optimal performance for skin disease detection.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform the development of automated diagnostic tools for skin diseases, including monkeypox, potentially improving early detection and classification in clinical settings.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence strength is moderate as the study was conducted on a specific dataset with limited classes, and the performance metrics, while high, may not generalize to all populations or skin disease types.

塞拉利昂和马诺河联盟的 猴痘:实时展开的流行病。

Mpox in Sierra Leone and the Mano River Union: an epidemic unfolding in real time.
作者： Nelson Aghogho Evaborhene, Sulaiman Lakoh, Darlinda F. Jiba, Chizaram Onyeaghala
摘要中文翻译2025 年塞拉利昂的 猴痘 暴发 代表了一场前所未有的公共卫生危机,在 6 个月内从先前的极少病例迅速升级到超过 4400 例确诊感染。这一激增挑战了之前关于西非 猴痘 流行病学 的假设,并突显了包括利比里亚、几内亚和科特迪瓦在内的马诺河联盟 (MRU) 地区在疾病 监测、诊断和卫生系统准备方面面临的严重脆弱性。尽管全球承诺实现公平的 疫苗 获取,但塞拉利昂及其邻国仍面临严重的延误和 疫苗 疫苗 部署的支离破碎,反映出全球卫生融资、疫苗 生态系统和治理方面的系统性失败。在本分析中,我们研究了塞拉利昂和 MRU 内 暴发 的人口和地理特征、运营挑战以及协调公平驱动的区域应对措施的迫切需要。为此,我们呼吁有针对性的疫苗接种、分散检测、将猴痘控制纳入性健康计划以及加强非洲疫苗的制造能力。暴发 强调了重新思考全球卫生治理的必要性,以确保为流行地区和相互关联的地区提供及时、持续的支持。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该审查审查了塞拉利昂和马诺河联盟 (MRU) 地区的 2025 年 猴痘 暴发,重点关注其在 6 个月内从极少的先前病例史无前例地升级到超过 4400 例确诊感染病例。核心问题涉及分析人口和地理特征、行动挑战以及协调区域应对措施的必要性。
证据基础与方法
该评论是对塞拉利昂和 MRU 地区的 猴痘 暴发 的叙述性分析。它不报告具体的数据库、检索日期、资格标准或研究计数,也不描述荟萃分析或共识过程。

审查的结论是,暴发 挑战了之前关于西非 猴痘 流行病学 的假设,强调了整个 MRU 在疾病 监测、诊断和卫生系统准备方面的漏洞。它将全球卫生融资、疫苗 生态系统和治理方面的系统性失败视为导致危机的关键因素。
争议与缺口
该审查确定了与塞拉利昂及其邻国延迟和分散的 猴痘 疫苗 部署相关的证据差距,反映了全球卫生治理的系统性失败。它强调需要重新考虑对流行地区和相互关联地区的全球卫生支持。
科研与实践启示
该审查呼吁有针对性的疫苗接种、分散测试、将猴痘控制纳入性健康计划以及加强非洲疫苗制造能力。它强调迫切需要采取协调一致、公平驱动的区域应对措施,并重新思考全球卫生治理,以确保及时、持续的支持。

Mpox in Sierra Leone and the Mano River Union: an epidemic unfolding in real time.

Authors: Nelson Aghogho Evaborhene, Sulaiman Lakoh, Darlinda F. Jiba, Chizaram Onyeaghala
Original abstract (English)
The 2025 mpox outbreak in Sierra Leone represents an unprecedented public health crisis, escalating rapidly from minimal prior cases to over 4400 confirmed infections within 6 months. This surge challenges previous assumptions about mpox epidemiology in West Africa and highlights critical vulnerabilities in disease surveillance, diagnostics and health system preparedness-challenges shared across the Mano River Union (MRU) region, including Liberia, Guinea and Côte d'Ivoire. Despite global commitments to equitable vaccine access, Sierra Leone and its neighbours have faced significant delays and fragmented mpox vaccine deployment, reflecting systemic failures in global health financing, vaccine ecosystems and governance. In this analysis, we examine the demographic and geographic features of the outbreak within Sierra Leone and the MRU, operational challenges and the urgent need for a coordinated equity-driven regional response. In doing so, we call for targeted vaccination, decentralised testing, the integration of mpox control into sexual health programmes and the strengthening of African vaccine manufacturing capacity. The outbreak underscores the necessity of rethinking global health governance to ensure timely sustained support for endemic and interconnected regions.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the 2025 mpox outbreak in Sierra Leone and the Mano River Union (MRU) region, focusing on its unprecedented escalation from minimal prior cases to over 4400 confirmed infections within 6 months. The central question involves analyzing demographic and geographic features, operational challenges, and the need for a coordinated regional response.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative analysis examining the mpox outbreak in Sierra Leone and the MRU region. It does not report specific databases, search dates, eligibility criteria, or study counts, nor does it describe a meta-analysis or consensus process.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that the outbreak challenges previous assumptions about mpox epidemiology in West Africa, highlighting vulnerabilities in disease surveillance, diagnostics, and health system preparedness across the MRU. It identifies systemic failures in global health financing, vaccine ecosystems, and governance as key factors contributing to the crisis.
Controversies and gaps
The review identifies evidence gaps related to the delayed and fragmented mpox vaccine deployment in Sierra Leone and neighboring countries, reflecting systemic failures in global health governance. It underscores the need for rethinking global health support for endemic and interconnected regions.
Research and practice implications
The review calls for targeted vaccination, decentralized testing, integration of mpox control into sexual health programs, and strengthening African vaccine manufacturing capacity. It emphasizes the urgent need for a coordinated equity-driven regional response and rethinking global health governance to ensure timely sustained support.

免疫功能障碍导致人类免疫缺陷病毒感染者出现不良 猴痘 结果:基于 CD4 的风险分层的系统评价和荟萃分析

Immune dysfunction drives adverse mpox outcomes among people living with human immunodeficiency virus: a systematic review and meta-analysis with CD4-based risk stratification
作者： Chunting Qiu, De-fa Zhang, Ziyu Wang, Lina Fan, Ping Ma
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注 HIV 感染者 (PLWHIV) 中的 猴痘病毒 (MPXV) 及其疾病 (猴痘),特别研究不良结果是否可归因于 HIV 感染本身或免疫功能障碍的程度。核心问题是免疫状态和 HIV 疾病控制是否比单独的 HIV 血清状态更好地解释 猴痘 不良结果。
证据基础与方法
根据 PRISMA 2020 和 Cochrane 方法进行系统评价和荟萃分析。PubMed、Embase 和 Web of Science 的搜索时间为从开始到 2026 年 1 月 31 日,有针对性的更新截至 2026 年 5 月 27 日。报告按 HIV 状态分层的 猴痘 结果的观察性研究符合条件,住院作为主要结果。使用 DerSimonian-Laird 随机效应模型汇总风险比 (RR)。定性综合中纳入了 20 项研究,住院荟萃分析中纳入了 13 项研究,涉及 32,685 名参与者。
共识与结论
HIV infection was associated with increased hospitalization risk (RR, 1.40; 95% CI, 1.07–1.85; I2 = 81%). The wide 95% prediction interval (0.54–3.65) indicates that context, case mix, and hospitalization thresholds influenced observed effects. Available studies consistently indicate that low CD4 count, uncontrolled HIV viremia, and disengagement from HIV care identify the subgroup at greatest risk for hospitalization, severe disease, prolonged infection, and death. Adverse mpox outcomes among PLWHIV are better explained by immune status and HIV disease control than by HIV serostatus alone.
争议与缺口
CD4 分层证据对于有效的汇总 CD4 特异性效应估计来说不够统一。高异质性 (I2 = 81%) 和宽预测区间表明不同环境和人群中 HIV 和 猴痘 结果之间的关联存在很大差异。该综述并未涉及潜在的混杂因素或抗逆转录病毒治疗对结果的影响。
科研与实践启示
基于 CD4 的风险分层为分诊、随访强度、抗病毒药物-治疗 考虑以及 猴痘 管理与 HIV 护理的整合提供了临床上可行的框架。未来的研究应侧重于标准化 CD4 分层分析并调查 HIV 控制措施对 猴痘 结果的影响。

Immune dysfunction drives adverse mpox outcomes among people living with human immunodeficiency virus: a systematic review and meta-analysis with CD4-based risk stratification

Authors: Chunting Qiu, De-fa Zhang, Ziyu Wang, Lina Fan, Ping Ma
Original abstract (English)
Background People living with human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) accounted for a large share of cases during the 2022 multinational mpox outbreak, but whether adverse outcomes are attributable to HIV infection itself or to the degree of immune dysfunction remains uncertain. Methods We conducted a systematic review and meta-analysis according to PRISMA 2020 and Cochrane methods. PubMed, Embase, and Web of Science were searched from inception through January 31, 2026, with targeted literature updating through May 27, 2026. Observational studies reporting mpox outcomes stratified by HIV status were eligible. The primary outcome was hospitalization. Risk ratios (RRs) were pooled using DerSimonian-Laird random-effects models. Results Twenty studies were included in the qualitative synthesis and 13 studies comprising 32,685 participants were included in the hospitalization meta-analysis. HIV infection was associated with increased hospitalization risk (RR, 1.40; 95% CI, 1.07–1.85; I 2 = 81%). The 95% prediction interval was wide (0.54–3.65), indicating that context, case mix, and hospitalization thresholds influenced observed effects. CD4-stratified evidence was not sufficiently uniform for a valid pooled CD4-specific effect estimate, but available studies consistently indicated that low CD4 count, uncontrolled HIV viremia, and disengagement from HIV care identify the subgroup at greatest risk for hospitalization, severe disease, prolonged infection, and death. Conclusion Adverse mpox outcomes among people living with HIV are better explained by immune status and HIV disease control than by HIV serostatus alone. CD4-based risk stratification provides a clinically actionable framework for triage, follow-up intensity, antiviral-treatment consideration, and integration of mpox management with HIV care.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on mpox virus (MPXV) and its disease (mpox) in people living with HIV (PLWHIV), specifically examining whether adverse outcomes are attributable to HIV infection itself or to the degree of immune dysfunction. The central question is whether immune status and HIV disease control better explain adverse mpox outcomes than HIV serostatus alone.
Evidence base and method
A systematic review and meta-analysis was conducted according to PRISMA 2020 and Cochrane methods. PubMed, Embase, and Web of Science were searched from inception through January 31, 2026, with targeted updates through May 27, 2026. Observational studies reporting mpox outcomes stratified by HIV status were eligible, with hospitalization as the primary outcome. Risk ratios (RRs) were pooled using DerSimonian-Laird random-effects models. Twenty studies were included in the qualitative synthesis, and 13 studies with 32,685 participants in the hospitalization meta-analysis.
Consensus and conclusions
HIV infection was associated with increased hospitalization risk (RR, 1.40; 95% CI, 1.07–1.85; I2 = 81%). The wide 95% prediction interval (0.54–3.65) indicates that context, case mix, and hospitalization thresholds influenced observed effects. Available studies consistently indicate that low CD4 count, uncontrolled HIV viremia, and disengagement from HIV care identify the subgroup at greatest risk for hospitalization, severe disease, prolonged infection, and death. Adverse mpox outcomes among PLWHIV are better explained by immune status and HIV disease control than by HIV serostatus alone.
Controversies and gaps
CD4-stratified evidence was not sufficiently uniform for a valid pooled CD4-specific effect estimate. The high heterogeneity (I2 = 81%) and wide prediction interval suggest substantial variability in the association between HIV and mpox outcomes across different settings and populations. The review does not address potential confounding factors or the impact of antiretroviral therapy on outcomes.
Research and practice implications
CD4-based risk stratification provides a clinically actionable framework for triage, follow-up intensity, antiviral-treatment consideration, and integration of mpox management with HIV care. Future research should focus on standardizing CD4-stratified analyses and investigating the impact of HIV control measures on mpox outcomes.

猴痘病毒 传播 检疫建模:对结构定义、参数证据和策略推断的批判性评估

Modelling Quarantine in Monkeypox Virus Transmission: A Critical Appraisal of Construct Definition, Parameter Evidence and Policy Inference
作者： P. O. Aye, P. O. Olatunji, F. T. Daodu, O. J. Olatubi, S. A. Jegede
摘要中文翻译检疫在 猴痘 的定量文献中占据着不寻常的地位。它是猴痘病毒 (MPXV) 传播 模型中最常见的模拟控制措施之一,但也是最不精确的指定措施之一。2022年由进化枝IIb驱动的多国暴发,以及随后进化枝I 传播在中非和东非的扩张,促进了分区、网络和基于个人的模型的迅速扩散,其中使用隔离等级或隔离率来表示将暴露或感染性个体从有效接触中移除。本综述研究了如何在文献中定义、参数化、校准和解释隔离结构,并询问由此产生的政策主张是否得到其所依据的证据的支持。通过可访问的学术索引和元数据服务的结构化搜索来识别文献,并辅以向后和向前的引文搜索以及权威机构来源的咨询,并根据涵盖结构有效性、结构假设、参数出处、校准、不确定性治疗和推理逻辑的标准进行评估。出现了六项重要发现。对暴露接触者的检疫和有症状病例的隔离通常会混为一谈,因此报告的效应大小指的是单一标签下的不同干预措施。尽管 MPXV 传播 具有强烈的过度分散性和网络结构,但确定性同质混合架构占主导地位。隔离参数通常是假设的而不是估计的,敏感性分析经常是在发明的范围而不是经验范围内进行的。提高检疫率会降低繁殖数量这一反复出现的结果很大程度上是模型制定的结构性结果,而不是经验发现。数据锚定分析比参数扫描分析得出更多有条件的结论。对隔离和接触者追踪的实证评估表明,效果的确定性很弱,并且操作上存在重大失败,特别是在流行环境中。隔离有效性取决于分支、传播 路线、检测延迟和遵守情况,而忽略这些意外事件的模型夸大了隔离可以实现的效果。优先事项包括构建标准化、检测和隔离延迟的联合估计以及遵守和公平约束的明确表示。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述考察了有关猴痘病毒 (MPXV) 传播 模型检疫的定量文献,重点关注构造定义、参数证据和政策推断。核心问题是有关检疫有效性的政策主张是否得到基础证据的支持。
证据基础与方法
该综述采用了学术索引和元数据服务的结构化搜索,并辅以向后和向前的引文搜索以及权威机构资源的咨询。根据涵盖构造有效性、结构假设、参数来源、校准、不确定性 治疗 和推理逻辑的标准对文献进行评估。
共识与结论
出现了六项重要发现:对暴露接触者的隔离和对有症状病例的隔离通常被混为一谈;尽管 MPXV 的 传播 过度分散,但确定性均匀混合模型仍占主导地位;检疫参数通常是假设的而不是估计的;检疫率对繁殖数量的影响很大程度上是结构性后果;数据锚定分析比参数扫描分析产生更多有条件的结论;实证评估报告效果确定性较弱且操作失败,特别是在流行情况下。
争议与缺口
主要争议包括检疫和隔离结构的合并、无法捕获 MPXV 的 传播 动态的简单化模型结构的主导地位,以及对假设参数而不是估计参数的依赖。证据差距包括缺乏敏感性分析的经验范围,对遵守和公平约束的代表性不足,以及对影响检疫有效性的特定进化枝、传播 路线特定和环境特定因素考虑不足。
科研与实践启示
优先事项包括标准化隔离结构、联合估计检测和隔离延迟以及在模型中明确表示遵守和公平约束。研究结果表明,应谨慎解释有关检疫有效性的政策主张,特别是在基于简单化模型或假设参数时,并且需要更加针对具体情况、以经验为基础的方法。

Modelling Quarantine in Monkeypox Virus Transmission: A Critical Appraisal of Construct Definition, Parameter Evidence and Policy Inference

Authors: P. O. Aye, P. O. Olatunji, F. T. Daodu, O. J. Olatubi, S. A. Jegede
Original abstract (English)
Quarantine occupies an unusual position in the quantitative literature on mpox. It is among the most frequently simulated control measures in transmission models of monkeypox virus (MPXV), yet it is among the least precisely specified. The 2022 multi-country outbreak driven by clade IIb, and the subsequent expansion of clade I transmission in central and eastern Africa, prompted a rapid proliferation of compartmental, network and individual-based models in which a quarantine class or a quarantine rate is used to represent the removal of exposed or infectious individuals from effective contact. This review examines how the quarantine construct has been defined, parameterised, calibrated and interpreted across that literature, and asks whether the resulting policy claims are supported by the evidence on which they rest. Literature was identified through structured searches of accessible scholarly indexes and metadata services, supplemented by backward and forward citation searching and by consultation of authoritative institutional sources, and was appraised against criteria covering construct validity, structural assumptions, parameter provenance, calibration, uncertainty treatment and inferential logic. Six critical findings emerge. Quarantine of exposed contacts and isolation of symptomatic cases are routinely conflated, so that reported effect sizes refer to different interventions under a single label. Deterministic homogeneous-mixing architectures dominate, although MPXV transmission is strongly overdispersed and network-structured. Quarantine parameters are typically assumed rather than estimated, and sensitivity analyses are frequently conducted over invented rather than empirical ranges. The recurrent result that raising the quarantine rate reduces the reproduction number is largely a structural consequence of model formulation rather than an empirical discovery. Data-anchored analyses reach substantially more conditional conclusions than parameter-sweep analyses. Empirical evaluations of isolation and contact tracing report weak certainty of effect and substantial operational failure, particularly in endemic settings. Quarantine effectiveness is contingent on clade, transmission route, detection delay and adherence, and models that omit these contingencies overstate what quarantine can achieve. Priorities include construct standardisation, joint estimation of detection and isolation delays, and explicit representation of adherence and equity constraints.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review examines the quantitative literature on quarantine in monkeypox virus (MPXV) transmission models, focusing on construct definition, parameter evidence, and policy inference. The central question is whether policy claims about quarantine effectiveness are supported by the underlying evidence.
Evidence base and method
The review used structured searches of scholarly indexes and metadata services, supplemented by backward and forward citation searching and consultation of authoritative institutional sources. Literature was appraised against criteria covering construct validity, structural assumptions, parameter provenance, calibration, uncertainty treatment, and inferential logic.
Consensus and conclusions
Six critical findings emerge: quarantine of exposed contacts and isolation of symptomatic cases are routinely conflated; deterministic homogeneous-mixing models dominate despite MPXV's overdispersed transmission; quarantine parameters are typically assumed rather than estimated; the effect of quarantine rates on reproduction numbers is largely a structural consequence; data-anchored analyses yield more conditional conclusions than parameter-sweep analyses; and empirical evaluations report weak certainty of effect and operational failure, particularly in endemic settings.
Controversies and gaps
Key controversies include the conflation of quarantine and isolation constructs, the dominance of simplistic model structures that fail to capture MPXV's transmission dynamics, and the reliance on assumed rather than estimated parameters. Evidence gaps include the lack of empirical ranges for sensitivity analyses, insufficient representation of adherence and equity constraints, and inadequate consideration of clade-specific, transmission route-specific, and context-specific factors affecting quarantine effectiveness.
Research and practice implications
Priorities include standardizing quarantine constructs, jointly estimating detection and isolation delays, and explicitly representing adherence and equity constraints in models. The findings suggest that policy claims about quarantine effectiveness should be interpreted with caution, particularly when based on simplistic models or assumed parameters, and that more context-specific, empirically grounded approaches are needed.

应对流行病准备的数字健康:对传染病创新、公平差距和治理的批判性回顾 监测

Digital Health for Pandemic Preparedness: A Critical Review of Innovations, Equity Gaps, and Governance in Infectious Disease Surveillance
作者： Abdolreza Babamahmoodi, F. Babamahmoodi, Majid Marjani
摘要中文翻译背景:最近的传染病紧急事件,包括SARS-CoV-2、猴痘(2022-2024年)、美国奶牛中的高致病性禽流感A(H5N1)(2024-2025年)以及卢旺达的马尔堡病毒(2024年),都对传统的监测系统造成了压力。数字健康已被提出作为一种范式转变;然而,大多数评论都集中在诊断准确性上,而忽视了公平性和治理。证据获取:我们进行了一项结构化的主题叙事综述,涵盖了从2010年1月至2025年3月发表的同行评议文献和灰色文献,并按照PRISMA 2020和PRISMA-ScR进行报告。于2025年2月5日至18日检索了PubMed、Scopus和Web of Science。搜索关键词结合了三个概念块:(1)数字健康技术(人工智能、机器学习、移动健康、电子健康、远程医疗、电子健康记录、可穿戴设备、废水监测、基因组监测、自然语言处理);(2)传染病(暴发、大流行、流行病、COVID-19、埃博拉、寨卡、猴痘、流感、抗菌素耐药性、马尔堡);(3)公共卫生功能(监测、检测、预测、接触者追踪、治理、公平性)。提供了完整的布尔语法。在EndNote X20中去重记录,并由2名盲审评审人员在Rayyan中进行筛选(Cohen κ = 0.81)。使用混合方法评估工具2018评估实证研究,使用Joanna Briggs Institute清单评估综述。综合分析遵循Braun和Clarke的反身主题分析,并通过社会技术系统和数据正义视角进行解释。结果:在1498条记录中,142项研究符合纳入标准;48%(69/142)被评为高质量。证据基础在区域分布上严重偏向(高收入国家[HICs],41%;撒哈拉以南非洲或南亚,19%)。没有一种技术具有普遍优越性。人工智能(AI)研究集中在高收入国家(32项中的18项,56%),而短信服务(SMS)移动健康研究则集中在低收入和中等收入国家(LMICs;25项中的15项,60%)。反复出现的障碍包括算法偏见、碎片化的互操作性、依赖捐助者的可持续性,以及与低收入和中等收入国家分享病原体数据的缓慢利益共享。结论:在142项纳入研究的综合分析支持下,我们得出一个谨慎的结论:数字健康对于大流行防范是必要但不充分的。证据中最一致观察到的模式——区域偏向、反复出现的治理失败、依赖捐助者的可持续性,以及决定公平性结果的设计选择——共同表明,公平性、可持续性和问责制是有效性的组成部分,而非障碍。日益受到限制的是政治和制度意愿,而非分析不确定性。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该评论审查了大流行防范的数字健康创新,重点关注传染病 监测,核心问题是这些技术如何在最近的 暴发(如 猴痘)的背景下解决公平差距和治理挑战。
证据基础与方法
继 PRISMA 2020 和 PRISMA-ScR 之后,对 2010 年 1 月至 2025 年 3 月的同行评审和灰色文献进行结构化主题叙述性回顾。数据库包括 PubMed、Scopus 和 Web of Science,记录由两名盲审者筛选(Cohen κ = 0.81)。使用 2018 年混合方法评估工具对实证研究进行评估,并使用乔安娜·布里格斯研究所清单进行审查。
共识与结论
数字健康对于大流行病防范来说是必要的,但还不够。证据强调了区域偏差(41% 的高收入国家,19% 的撒哈拉以南非洲/南亚)、治理失败、依赖捐助者的可持续性以及影响公平的设计选择。没有一种技术是普遍优越的,人工智能集中在高收入国家,短信移动医疗则集中在中低收入国家。
争议与缺口
反复出现的障碍包括算法偏差、互操作性分散、与中低收入国家的病原体数据惠益分享缓慢以及技术采用方面的区域不平等。证据基础缺乏来自资源匮乏地区的代表性,并且未能系统地解决可持续性和问责制问题。
科研与实践启示
优先考虑数字卫生工具的公平设计和治理,改善与中低收入国家的数据共享机制,并解决捐助者的依赖问题。加强研究的区域代表性,并制定大流行 监测 系统的问责制和可持续性框架。

Digital Health for Pandemic Preparedness: A Critical Review of Innovations, Equity Gaps, and Governance in Infectious Disease Surveillance

Authors: Abdolreza Babamahmoodi, F. Babamahmoodi, Majid Marjani
Original abstract (English)
Context: Recent infectious disease emergencies, including SARS-CoV-2, mpox (2022 - 2024), highly pathogenic avian influenza A(H5N1) in US dairy cattle (2024 - 2025), and the Marburg virus in Rwanda (2024), have strained traditional surveillance systems. Digital health has been proposed as a paradigm shift; however, most reviews have focused on diagnostic accuracy while neglecting equity and governance. Evidence Acquisition: We conducted a structured thematic narrative review of peer-reviewed and grey literature published from January 2010 to March 2025, reported in accordance with PRISMA 2020 and PRISMA-ScR. PubMed, Scopus, and Web of Science were searched from February 5 to 18, 2025. Search keywords combined three concept blocks: (1) Digital-health technologies (artificial intelligence, machine learning, mHealth, eHealth, telemedicine, electronic health records, wearables, wastewater surveillance, genomic surveillance, natural-language processing); (2) infectious diseases (outbreak, pandemic, epidemic, COVID-19, Ebola, Zika, mpox, influenza, antimicrobial resistance, Marburg); and (3) public-health functions (surveillance, detection, forecasting, contact tracing, governance, equity). Full Boolean syntax is provided. Records were deduplicated in EndNote X20 and screened by 2 blinded reviewers in Rayyan (Cohen κ = 0.81). Empirical studies were appraised using the Mixed Methods Appraisal Tool 2018, and reviews were appraised using Joanna Briggs Institute checklists. The synthesis followed Braun and Clarke's reflexive thematic analysis and was interpreted through sociotechnical systems and data justice lenses. Results: Of 1498 records, 142 studies met the inclusion criteria; 48% (69/142) were rated as high quality. The evidence base was heavily skewed regionally (high-income countries [HICs], 41%; sub-Saharan Africa or South Asia, 19%). No technology was universally superior. Artificial intelligence (AI) studies were concentrated in HICs (18/32, 56%), whereas short-message-service (SMS) mHealth studies were concentrated in low- and middle-income countries (LMICs; 15/25, 60%). Recurring barriers included algorithmic bias, fragmented interoperability, donor-dependent sustainability, and slow benefit-sharing of pathogen data with LMICs. Conclusions: Across 142 included studies the synthesis supports a measured conclusion: Digital health is necessary but not sufficient for pandemic preparedness. The patterns most consistently observed in the evidence — regional skew, recurring governance failures, donor-dependent sustainability, and design choices that determine equity outcomes — together indicate that equity, sustainability, and accountability are constituents of effectiveness rather than obstacles to it. The binding constraint is increasingly political and institutional will rather than analytic uncertainty.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines digital health innovations for pandemic preparedness, focusing on infectious disease surveillance, with a central question on how these technologies address equity gaps and governance challenges in the context of recent outbreaks like mpox.
Evidence base and method
A structured thematic narrative review of peer-reviewed and grey literature from January 2010 to March 2025, following PRISMA 2020 and PRISMA-ScR. Databases included PubMed, Scopus, and Web of Science, with records screened by two blinded reviewers (Cohen κ = 0.81). Empirical studies were appraised using the Mixed Methods Appraisal Tool 2018, and reviews using Joanna Briggs Institute checklists.
Consensus and conclusions
Digital health is necessary but not sufficient for pandemic preparedness. The evidence highlights regional skew (41% HICs, 19% sub-Saharan Africa/South Asia), governance failures, donor-dependent sustainability, and design choices affecting equity. No technology was universally superior, with AI concentrated in HICs and SMS mHealth in LMICs.
Controversies and gaps
Recurring barriers include algorithmic bias, fragmented interoperability, slow benefit-sharing of pathogen data with LMICs, and regional inequities in technology adoption. The evidence base lacks representation from low-resource regions and fails to address sustainability and accountability systematically.
Research and practice implications
Prioritize equitable design and governance of digital health tools, improve data-sharing mechanisms with LMICs, and address donor dependency. Strengthen regional representation in research and develop frameworks for accountability and sustainability in pandemic surveillance systems.

I 型猴痘 暴发 - 疾病控制和预防中心 |疾病预防控制中心 (.gov)

Clade I Monkeypox Outbreaks - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)
新闻简报CDC 报告称,自 2023 年底以来,I 型猴痘病毒持续出现 暴发,主要发生在中非和东非,全球确诊病例超过 55,000 例,死亡人数超过 150 例。病例已通过旅行者传播到西欧、北美和其他地区,自 2026 年 3 月以来,美国已有 30 多例病例。Ib 亚支推动了大部分国际传播,在高收入国家表现出轻微疾病,但没有死亡。在美国,大多数人群的风险仍然较低,但男男性行为者的风险为低至中等。CDC 正在支持应对工作,包括 监测 和实验室能力,并预计会出现更多病例。传播 通过性接触、家庭接触和动物接触发生,由于家庭传播和医疗保健获取限制,非洲儿童受到的影响尤为严重。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026 年 7 月 28 日。事件时间范围:暴发 于 2023 年底开始,自 2026 年 3 月以来报告的病例有所增加。
地点与对象
主要集中在中非和东非(刚果民主共和国),随后蔓延至西欧、北美(美国)、亚洲、南美和澳大利亚的邻国和与旅行相关的病例。受影响的人群包括非洲的成人和儿童,以及高收入国家的旅行者/亲密接触者。
事件
确诊为 I 支猴痘病毒 (MPXV) 暴发,包括 Ia 和 Ib 支支,有实验室确诊病例,多个地区持续存在本地 传播。该事件由疾病预防控制中心正式报道。
规模、影响与风险
自 2024 年 1 月以来,全球已有超过 55,000 例确诊病例,150 多人死亡。截至 2026 年 7 月,美国报告了 30 多例病例,全部与旅行有关。对于大多数人群来说较低,对于男男性行为者来说则较低至中等。高收入国家没有死亡报告。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 正在支持受影响地区的应对工作,包括 监测、实验室能力建设、病例管理和感染控制。欧洲和美国预计还会出现更多病例。数据可能会发生变化,仅供态势感知之用。传播 动态正在演变,性接触和家庭接触被确定为传播途径。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Clade I Monkeypox Outbreaks - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)

News Brief
The CDC reports ongoing outbreaks of clade I monkeypox virus since late 2023, primarily in Central and Eastern Africa, with over 55,000 confirmed cases and 150+ deaths globally. Cases have spread to Western Europe, North America, and other regions via travelers, with over 30 cases in the U.S. since March 2026. Subclade Ib drives most international spread, showing mild disease with no deaths in high-income countries. Risk in the U.S. remains low for most populations but low to moderate for men who have sex with men. The CDC is supporting response efforts, including surveillance and lab capacity, and expects additional cases. Transmission occurs through sexual, household, and animal contact, with children in Africa disproportionately affected due to household spread and healthcare access limitations.

What to know There have been outbreaks of clade I monkeypox in Central and Eastern Africa since late 2023. Since January 2024, more than 55,000 confirmed cases of clade I monkeypox have been reported globally, including more than 150 deaths. Newer outbreaks in several Western European countries are likely related to this ongoing outbreak. Transmission dynamics are changing. Only a small proportion of exported clade I monkeypox cases worldwide this year have originated in Central or Eastern African countries. Recent travel-associated clade I monkeypox cases have almost exclusively been attributed to subclade Ib; there have been no deaths associated with these cases and available data have detailed relatively mild disease courses. Outbreak overview In the United States The risk posed by the clade I monkeypox outbreak to most people within the United States remains low. The risk to men who have sex with men is low to moderate. Clade I cases in the United States have increased in frequency since the beginning of March 2026. As of July 2026, there have been more than 30 clade I monkeypox cases reported in the United States. Nearly all U.S. cases were diagnosed in people who: Had recently traveled to areas associated with the outbreak in Central and Eastern Africa; Had recently traveled to parts of Western Europe where newer outbreaks are being reported; OR Were linked to people who had traveled from these areas. CDC expects additional cases in Europe and the United States. Preventing Monkeypox While Traveling Learn where monkeypox is spreading and how to lower your risk of getting it during travel. In Central and Eastern Africa Clade Ia monkeypox outbreaks began impacting western areas of the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) in 2024. Available data indicate that clade Ia monkeypox has spread through sexual contact, regular household contact, and encounters with infected dead or live wild animals. Clade Ib monkeypox cases initially impacted eastern areas of DRC in late 2023. This subclade has driven much of the spread throughout Central and Eastern Africa and most travel-associated cases to other parts of the world. Available data indicate that these cases at first spread through heterosexual intimate or sexual contact between adults, then spread likely occurred within households, including to children. The disease spread in 2024 and 2025 to neighboring countries of DRC, only some of which are endemic for the virus that causes monkeypox, and sustained local spread occurred. As of April 2026, the Democratic Republic of the Congo is still the country with the majority of clade Ib cases. Outside Central and Eastern Africa Several countries in other parts of Africa, Asia, Europe, North America, and South America, as well as Australia, have confirmed clade I monkeypox cases mostly in travelers who'd recently been in areas with clade I outbreaks. To date, there have been more than 300 clade Ib cases in high-income countries, and no deaths have been reported. Beginning in Fall 2025, several countries in Western Europe began reporting clade Ib monkeypox cases among individuals who had no documented history of international travel. These cases were likely related to intimate or sexual exposure among men who have sex with men. We expect additional cases in Europe and the United States. For data since January 1, 2024, see 2022-24 Monkeypox (Monkeypox) Outbreak: Global Trends (shinyapps.io). Confirmed cases include those that are laboratory confirmed as monkeypox virus and may include cases only confirmed as orthopoxvirus. These data are provided for situational awareness and are subject to change. Clade I monkeypox and children Early in the Central and Eastern African outbreak, a high proportion of suspected cases in some areas were reported in children younger than 15 years of age. Historically, cases of monkeypox in children where monkeypox occurs regularly often happen after contact with wild animals that carry the disease. People then can spread the disease to close contacts, including in their households. The number of children with suspected monkeypox in this outbreak is probably due to spread within and between close households, the large number of younger people in affected countries, misdiagnosis of other diseases (such as measles), poor sanitary conditions, limited access to health services, and a high level of food insecurity and malnutrition. Based on what we know today, we don't expect to see the same sort of pattern in the United States for several reasons, including smaller household sizes, access to cleaning and disinfecting products, and improved access to medical care, equipment, and information about preventing spread while caring for patients. Notably, there are no consistent reports of schools as drivers of monkeypox transmission during any recent outbreaks. If cases of clade I monkeypox do happen in children the United States, CDC has clinical and public health guidance for reporting, testing, and effective case management. What CDC is doing CDC has been supporting DRC monkeypox research and response for more than 20 years. CDC and other U.S. government agencies have continued working in-country with partners in affected areas on disease surveillance, building local laboratory capacity, strengthening public health and laboratory workforce capacity, case investigation, case management, infection prevention and control, border health, and risk communication and community engagement. CDC staff brought laboratory supplies and trained local staff on laboratory equipment Resources Monkeypox in the United States and Around the World: Current Situation There are outbreaks of clade I and clade II monkeypox around the world. View five news elements
Time
Publication date: July 28, 2026. Event timeframe: Outbreaks began in late 2023, with increased cases reported since March 2026.
Location and population
Primarily Central and Eastern Africa (Democratic Republic of the Congo), with spread to neighboring countries and travel-associated cases in Western Europe, North America (United States), Asia, South America, and Australia. Affected populations include adults and children in Africa, and travelers/intimate contacts in high-income countries.
Event
Confirmed outbreaks of clade I monkeypox virus (MPXV), including subclades Ia and Ib, with laboratory-confirmed cases and sustained local transmission in multiple regions. The event is officially reported by the CDC.
Scale, impact and risk
Globally, over 55,000 confirmed cases and more than 150 deaths since January 2024. In the United States, over 30 cases reported as of July 2026, all linked to travel. is low for most populations, low to moderate for men who have sex with men. No deaths reported in high-income countries.
Response, status and uncertainty
CDC is supporting response efforts in affected areas, including surveillance, laboratory capacity building, case management, and infection control. Additional cases are expected in Europe and the U.S. Data are subject to change and provided for situational awareness. Transmission dynamics are evolving, with sexual and household contact identified as routes of spread.

疾病预防控制中心的几内亚猴痘应对措施内部 - 疾病控制与预防中心疾病预防控制中心 (.gov)

Inside CDC's Guinea Monkeypox Response - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)
新闻简报2025 年 6 月,几内亚面临迄今为止最大规模的猴痘 暴发,第二支病例迅速从科纳克里蔓延到七个地区。截至 2025 年 12 月 11 日,报告了 1,894 例确诊病例和 5 例死亡,其中主要是通过亲密/性行为 传播 的成年男性。CDC 通过部署 FETP 毕业生和接触追踪人员、通过国际试剂资源增强实验室能力以及改进数据系统以加快决策速度来支持几内亚的应对工作。该回应强调了病例检测和敏感数据收集方面的差距,从而导致了有针对性的培训。事实证明,疾病预防控制中心与几内亚卫生系统长达十年的合作伙伴关系对于遏制 暴发 和降低全球传播风险至关重要。

时间
发布日期:2026年8月1日。活动期间:2025年6月至2025年12月11日(暴发持续时间)。
地点与对象
受影响地区:科纳克里(首都)、金迪亚和其他七个地区。受影响人群:成年男性,尤其是那些有密切接触的男性。
事件
几内亚卫生部在 CDC 支持下报告了确诊的猴痘 暴发(分支 II)。事件状态:已确认 暴发 并正在进行响应。
规模、影响与风险
病例:截至 2025 年 12 月 11 日,确诊病例为 1,894 例。传播:通过密切/性接触传播,从科纳克里扩展到七个地区,每周激增至 99 例。风险:据称可能致命,并有国际传播的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
回应:CDC 部署了 FETP 调查员、接触者追踪员和实验室专家;加强了监测、测试和数据系统。不确定性:需要更快的病例检测、改进性接触数据收集以及持续的实验室能力。来源限制:已审查完整的原始文章(content_status:完整)。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Inside CDC's Guinea Monkeypox Response - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)

News Brief
In June 2025, Guinea faced its largest monkeypox outbreak to date, with clade II cases rapidly spreading from Conakry to seven regions. By December 11, 2025, 1,894 confirmed cases and five deaths were reported, primarily among adult men via close/sexual transmission. CDC supported Guinea's response by deploying FETP graduates and contact tracers, enhancing laboratory capacity through the International Reagent Resource, and improving data systems for faster decision-making. The response highlighted gaps in case detection and sensitive data collection, leading to targeted trainings. CDC's decade-long partnership with Guinea's health systems proved critical in containing the outbreak and reducing global spread risks.
Original source text (English)

Global Health Protection Explore This Topic Search Search Clear Search Public Health What We Do Campaigns and Awareness Days Stories of Impact Resources View all search clear search Global Health Protection Menu clear search Public Health What We Do Campaigns and Awareness Days Stories of Impact Resources View All Home Related Topics Global Health What We Do Campaigns and Awareness Days Stories of Impact Resources View Menu Inside CDC's Guinea Monkeypox Response For Public Health July 31, 2026, visit link for details. At a glance CDC experts worked with Guinea partners to quickly detect, investigate, and respond to a monkeypox outbreak. Prior investments in workforce development and preparedness, including Field Epidemiology Training Program (FETP) training, ensured responders were ready to act when the outbreak emerged. Rapid coordination, strengthened surveillance systems, and community engagement limited transmission and build lasting capacity for future health threats. The Challenge CDC supported the deployment of FETP investigators who conducted contact tracing in affected districts in Conakry and Kindia. In June 2025, Guinea was facing its largest monkeypox outbreak to date. Monkeypox is a viral disease that spreads through close, often intimate contact, causing painful lesions and other symptoms, severe illness, and in some cases, death. What began as a small number of clade II monkeypox cases in Conakry, the nation's capital, soon began to spread. As the outbreak expanded, CDC deployed global health security experts who worked closely with monkeypox subject matter experts and national partners. On-the-ground support helped to rapidly detect, track, and contain the outbreak at its source, moving quickly to contain a potentially deadly virus. Through a coordinated response, CDC played a critical role in slowing transmission, limiting further spread, and reducing the risk of the virus reaching the United States — helping protect communities both abroad and at home. Responding to outbreaks like monkeypox relies on consistent, coordinated efforts from early detection to contact tracing and laboratory confirmation. Together, these steps help reduce spread and protect communities. - Els Mathieu, MD, MPH, CDC Guinea Country Director A Rapidly Expanding Outbreak Early infections were largely among adult men, who often presented with visible monkeypox lesions. Available data suggested the outbreak was spreading through sexual transmission, like patterns seen in neighboring West African countries affected by recent clade I outbreaks. Within weeks, cases spread beyond Conakry into seven regions, with weekly totals surging to as many as 99 nationwide. By December 11, 2025, Guinea had reported 1,894 confirmed cases, including five deaths. The outbreak highlighted the urgent need to find cases faster and strengthen laboratory testing capacity for quicker and more accurate results. It also underscored the importance of tracking individuals who may have been exposed and better understanding how monkeypox was spreading within communities. The situation demanded speed, precision and coordination. Skin lesions on a patient with monkeypox. CDC Support on the Front Lines For more than a decade, CDC had been working with Guinea's Ministry of Health as a trusted partner strengthening disease surveillance, laboratory systems and outbreak response capacity. When the Ministry of Health asked CDC to assist with the outbreak, CDC's experts stepped up. CDC in collaboration with the Ministry of Health, the National Institute of Public Health (INSP), and the National Health Security Agency (ANSS), focused on what mattered most in the moment. Teams worked to find suspected cases sooner, confirm them quicker, and strengthen contact tracing so potential exposures didn't slip through the cracks. They also connected data across teams, turning scattered information into a clearer picture of how the outbreak was unfolding, helping responders move faster. Outbreaks like this don't stop at borders; they require coordination, trust, and real-time action. This response is a reminder that strengthening systems on the ground isn't just about infrastructure — it's about helping countries move faster, act earlier, and ultimately save lives. - CDC Guinea Regional Associate Director Kathleen Gallagher, D.Sc., MPH CDC supported the deployment of eight Field Epidemiology Training Program (FETP) graduates and 18 contact tracers to the most affected districts in Conakry and Kindia over a 12-week period. Both graduates and contact tracers worked case by case — identifying infections, tracing contacts, and helping local health workers respond quickly. Each connection mapped meant another opportunity to stop the virus from spreading further. Strengthening Laboratory Capacity Became Critical CDC Guinea, working with CDC poxvirus laboratory experts, provided technical support to the National Institute of Public Health (INSP). This included helping the laboratory connect to the International Reagent Resource (IRR) platform to directly access the reagents and supplies needed to detect and confirm monkeypox cases. CDC poxvirus laboratory experts also provided virtual training on how to use diagnostic tests capable of identifying viruses like monkeypox, and ensured the laboratories had the tools and supplies needed to carry out testing. Together, this support strengthened INSP's independence, enabling the laboratory to manage its own supply orders and sustain testing capacity without relying on external coordination. Behind the scenes, stronger data systems helped turn information into action. Through support to implementing partner, AFENET, Guinea expanded its use of DHIS2 to track monkeypox cases and strengthen coordination across national response teams, resulting in clearer situational awareness and faster, more targeted decision-making. Closing the Investigation Gap CDC's global health security experts supported a national training that brought together 20 experts from across Guinea to further strengthen the monkeypox response. As the outbreak evolved so did the response. By December, CDC identified a critical gap: the need to better understand how monkeypox was spreading through close and sexual contact, and who was most at risk. Early investigations showed that many cases involved close and sometimes intimate contact, but this information was not being consistently or safely collected. In response, CDC's global health security experts supported a national training that brought together 20 experts from across Guinea to strengthen how sensitive information such as sexual history and close contact exposure was collected during case investigations. The training focused on building skills to ask these questions respectfully, confidentially, and effectively, while collecting information essential for public health action. Those trained are now equipped to extend this knowledge across all 38 of the country's districts, further strengthening outbreak investigations across the nation. Impact That Reaches Beyond Borders Within weeks of the response launch, the trajectory began to shift. Transmission began to slow as districts brought cases under control and chains of infection were interrupted. What had been a rapidly expanding outbreak was contained through coordinated action, local leadership, and sustained technical support. CDC support also extended beyond the immediate response to strengthen core systems needed to sustain and scale Guinea's monkeypox response. This included the work CDC had done improving surveillance and contact tracing, building laboratory testing capacity, and equipping health workers to conduct more effective case investigations. As a result, several districts were able to exit active outbreak status, while stronger data collection enabled more targeted and effective public. Looking Ahead Slowing monkeypox in Guinea not only protected local communities but also helped stop the further spread of the virus and reduce its impact globally, including in the US. Future global health security efforts in the country will focus on expanding training, strengthening integrated surveillance systems, and ensuring Guinea is better prepared to respond quickly to any future outbreaks. Across dozens of countries, CDC is working side by side with partners to detect outbreaks early, respond rapidly, and stop threats before they escalate. In a connected world where disease travels quickly, containing outbreaks at their source reduces the risk of international spread. It's the kind of response and preparedness work that helps protect lives around the world and here at home. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Atypical Presentation of Mpox as Acute Tonsillitis: A Case Report from the United Arab Emirates - Cureus

News Brief
A previously healthy 28-year-old man in the United Arab Emirates presented with severe acute tonsillitis in January 2026, initially diagnosed as bacterial infection. Despite negative bacterial cultures and elevated inflammatory markers, mpox was only confirmed after characteristic cutaneous lesions developed post-admission. CT imaging revealed significant oropharyngeal narrowing, and real-time PCR confirmed mpox virus infection. The patient was isolated, transferred to a designated facility, and discharged after 27 days with full recovery. This case highlights the risk of diagnostic delay in mpox cases presenting with atypical oropharyngeal manifestations, particularly in regions with limited published descriptions of such presentations.
Original source text (English)
Mpox can present initially as severe pharyngotonsillitis with significant oropharyngeal luminal narrowing, mimicking acute bacterial infection and preceding the appearance of skin lesions by several days, even in young, immunocompetent patients without recognised epidemiological risk factors. Clinicians should consider Mpox in patients with severe pharyngotonsillitis, particularly when bacterial cultures are negative or when even subtle cutaneous changes appear during admission, to enable timely isolation and appropriate management. A previously healthy 28-year-old man in the United Arab Emirates presented with a two-day history of severe sore throat and painful swallowing that prevented oral intake. Examination revealed bilateral erythematous palatine tonsils with cryptic exudate, an edematous uvula, and pharyngeal erythema. There was no stridor or respiratory distress. Inflammatory markers were elevated (WBC count 14.0 × 109/L; CRP 102 mg/L). Computed tomography of the neck demonstrated bulky, heterogeneous palatine tonsils with significant narrowing of the oropharyngeal lumen and reactive cervical lymphadenopathy; the radiologist’s impression raised the possibility of evolving abscess. A working diagnosis of acute bacterial tonsillitis was made, and intravenous ceftriaxone and dexamethasone were commenced. On hospital day 2, mild non-specific cutaneous lesions appeared, progressing on day 3 to a characteristic vesiculopustular eruption involving the face, trunk, palms, and genital area. Throat culture, finalised on day 4, yielded normal upper respiratory tract flora with no beta-haemolytic streptococci. Real-time PCR on a skin lesion swab confirmed Mpox virus infection (cycle threshold 12). The patient was isolated, transferred to a designated facility on hospital day 9, and discharged home on 11 January 2026 after a total of 27 days from initial admission. A structured post-discharge telephone follow-up on 12 January 2026 confirmed sustained clinical recovery with full lesion resolution and no recurrent symptoms. Mpox typically presents with a prodrome of fever and lymphadenopathy followed by a characteristic vesiculopustular rash. Mpox may present with atypical manifestations, including isolated oropharyngeal, anogenital, and mucosal involvement preceding cutaneous lesions. Recognition of these atypical phenotypes is essential to avoid diagnostic delay and limit secondary transmission, particularly in regions such as the United Arab Emirates and the wider Gulf where published descriptions of atypical presentations remain limited. Introduction Mpox is a zoonotic orthopoxvirus infection that has historically been confined to endemic regions of Central and West Africa. Since May 2022, sustained human-to-human transmission has driven a global outbreak affecting more than 100 countries, prompting the World Health Organization to declare a Public Health Emergency of International Concern in 2022 and again in 2024 following the emergence of clade Ib in central Africa [1,2]. In the United Arab Emirates and the wider Gulf region, sporadic cases have been reported, predominantly linked to international travel [3]; published descriptions of locally relevant atypical clinical presentations remain limited. The classical clinical course consists of a febrile prodrome with lymphadenopathy followed within one to four days by a centrifugal vesiculopustular eruption that progresses through well-defined stages [4]. The 2022 outbreak, however, has been characterized by frequent atypical presentations. Mucosal involvement, including proctitis, urethritis, pharyngitis, and tonsillitis [5, 6], has been described, often preceding or occurring without the classical rash distribution. Severe oropharyngeal lesions causing odynophagia, dysphagia, and significant local inflammation have been documented in case series of clade IIb infection [7]. Such atypical presentations create a real risk of diagnostic delay. Patients may be admitted under alternative working diagnoses, most commonly acute bacterial tonsillitis or peritonsillar infection, and treated with empirical antibacterial therapy and adjunctive corticosteroids before Mpox is considered. This carries implications both for individual patient care and for infection control, as undiagnosed cases may expose healthcare workers and other patients before appropriate isolation is implemented. We report a case of laboratory-confirmed Mpox in a previously healthy, young man in the United Arab Emirates who presented with severe acute tonsillitis and CT evidence of significant oropharyngeal luminal narrowing, in whom the characteristic cutaneous eruption developed only after admission and after initial empirical antibacterial therapy. This case report was prepared in accordance with the CARE (CAse REport) Guidelines [2]. We aim to highlight the clinical features that should prompt consideration of Mpox in patients presenting with severe pharyngotonsillitis, including those without recognized epidemiological risk factors, and to discuss the practical infection-control implications of delayed recognition. To our knowledge, published reports from the United Arab Emirates describing Mpox presenting initially as severe acute tonsillitis with CT-confirmed oropharyngeal narrowing and without clinical airway compromise remain limited. Case Presentation A 28-year-old previously healthy man presented to the emergency department of a regional hospital in the United Arab Emirates with a two-day history of severe sore throat, progressive odynophagia, and inability to tolerate oral intake. He reported no fever, cough, dyspnea, rash, genital symptoms, or gastrointestinal complaints at presentation. He had no chronic medical conditions, took no regular medications, and reported no prior surgeries or known allergies. A detailed epidemiological exposure assessment was performed. The patient denied recent international travel, contact with individuals known to have Mpox or unexplained rash illnesses, healthcare-related exposure, attendance at mass gatherings, overnight stays in shared accommodation or hotels, occupational exposure to potentially infected individuals, recent domestic travel within the United Arab Emirates, and recognized sexual risk factors. Despite comprehensive exposure assessment, no definitive epidemiological source of infection was identified. The patient was alert and hemodynamically stable. Temperature was 37.2°C, heart rate 102 beats per minute, blood pressure 120/91 mmHg, and oxygen saturation 99% on room air. The otolaryngology examination documented bilateral erythematous palatine tonsils with cryptic exudate and no abscess on direct visualization, an erythematous and edematous uvula without deviation, and erythematous pharyngeal mucosa. The oral mucosa was non-erythematous. There was no trismus, no stridor, no respiratory distress, and no drooling. Bilateral tender cervical lymphadenopathy was noted. The skin was documented as moist with no rash at the time of initial examination. Flexible laryngoscopy was not performed. Laboratory studies on admission showed a leucocytosis of 14.0 × 109/L (neutrophils 7.74 × 109/L; lymphocytes 4.63 × 109/L; monocytes 1.25 × 109/L), platelet count 133 × 109/L, and elevated CRP of 102 mg/L. A throat swab obtained at admission in the emergency department was sent for bacterial culture. Given the severity of the progressive odynophagia and inability to tolerate oral intake, CT of the neck was performed to assess for a possible deep neck complication, including an evolving peritonsillar abscess. CT of the neck demonstrated bulky, heterogeneous bilateral palatine tonsils with several ill-defined hypodense areas within and significant narrowing of the oropharyngeal lumen (Figure 1). Multiple enlarged cervical lymph nodes were noted, the largest measuring 14 mm at level III. The nasopharynx, epiglottis, aryepiglottic folds, vallecula, pyriform sinuses, vocal cords, laryngeal cartilages, and visualized trachea and esophagus were reported as normal. The radiologist’s impression was “features of tonsillitis with the possibility of evolving abscess.” No follow-up imaging was performed during the admission. Figure 1: CT of the neck on admission An axial non-contrast CT image at the level of the oropharynx demonstrating bulky, heterogeneous bilateral palatine tonsils with ill-defined hypodense areas and significant narrowing of the oropharyngeal lumen. No discrete abscess is identified. Reactive cervical lymphadenopathy was noted on adjacent slices (not shown). The radiologist’s impression was “features of tonsillitis with the possibility of evolving abscess.” The differential diagnosis included acute bacterial tonsillitis, evolving peritonsillar abscess, infectious mononucleosis (Epstein-Barr virus), cytomegalovirus infection, acute HIV seroconversion syndrome, herpes simplex virus infection, Coxsackievirus infection, gonococcal pharyngitis, and other viral causes of severe pharyngotonsillitis. These conditions were considered because of the patient’s prominent oropharyngeal symptoms, elevated inflammatory markers, and the absence of characteristic cutaneous lesions at presentation. The working diagnosis was acute bacterial tonsillitis with possible evolving peritonsillar abscess, and the patient was admitted under joint internal medicine and otolaryngology care. Empirical intravenous ceftriaxone 1 g twice daily and intravenous dexamethasone 8 mg daily were commenced, alongside intravenous fluid support and analgesia. Conservative airway monitoring was implemented; given the absence of stridor or respiratory distress, no airway intervention was indicated. On hospital day 2, the patient developed mild, non-specific cutaneous lesions that did not initially suggest a defined diagnosis. On hospital day 3, the eruption progressed and became clinically characteristic: vesiculopustular lesions appeared on the face, trunk, back, upper limbs, and genital area, evolving sequentially through macular, papular, vesicular, and pustular stages, with several lesions showing central umbilication. Notably, lesions were also present on the palms and dorsa of the hands. The morphology and distribution prompted clinical suspicion of Mpox. The patient was placed in single-room isolation with contact and droplet precautions. A lesion swab was obtained for orthopoxvirus and mpox virus testing. On hospital day 4, the throat swab culture was finalized and reported as the growth of normal upper respiratory tract flora only, with no beta-hemolytic streptococci isolated. On hospital day 6, HIV antigen/antibody combination screening returned negative. Epstein-Barr virus testing showed negative viral capsid antigen IgM, positive viral capsid antigen IgG and nuclear antigen IgG, and undetectable Epstein-Barr virus PCR. Although infectious mononucleosis was considered initially, the emergence of characteristic cutaneous lesions prompted targeted Mpox testing. On hospital day 7, real-time PCR on the skin lesion swab returned positive for the Mpox virus, with a cycle threshold value of 12, consistent with a high viral load. The qualitative assay targeted Mpox virus and other orthopoxviruses using specific primers and probes, with internal and external controls to ensure reliability and detect potential inhibition. Clade-specific identification and additional sexually transmitted infection screening were not performed at our facility. Tecovirimat was not administered because the patient demonstrated progressive clinical improvement with supportive management and local institutional protocols did not recommend antiviral therapy in this setting. Additional sexually transmitted infection screening beyond HIV was not performed because comprehensive sexually transmitted infection testing was not routinely available within our facility at the time of admission and the patient denied recognized sexual risk factors; nevertheless, coinfections with sexually transmitted infections have been reported in Mpox outbreak cohorts and therefore cannot be completely excluded. The patient remained haemodynamically stable throughout admission, with no respiratory deterioration and no requirement for airway intervention. Tonsillar inflammation and oropharyngeal symptoms gradually improved with supportive care, and oral intake was progressively re-established. Cutaneous lesions evolved through pustular and crusting stages without secondary bacterial infection or other complications. Following laboratory confirmation of Mpox on hospital day 7, dedicated written informed consent for clinical photography and publication was obtained from the patient. Representative clinical photographs of the oropharyngeal (Figure 2) and cutaneous (Figure 3) lesions were subsequently obtained on hospital day 9, prior to transfer to the designated isolation facility on the same day. On hospital day 9, in line with national public health protocols, the patient was transferred to a designated Mpox isolation facility for ongoing care. He was discharged home on hospital day 27 in stable clinical condition, with complete resolution of pharyngeal symptoms and progressive healing of cutaneous lesions. Discharge instructions included continued isolation for several days, personal hygiene measures, and clear advice to return if symptoms recurred. A structured post-discharge telephone follow-up was completed on post-discharge day 1; the patient reported feeling well, with all lesions healed, no new lesions, and no recurrent symptoms. The total duration from initial admission to final discharge was 27 days, and no long-term complications were identified. Figure 2: Oropharyngeal lesions Clinical photograph of the oropharynx obtained on hospital day 9, following laboratory confirmation of Mpox and after dedicated written informed consent for clinical photography and publication had been obtained from the patient. The image shows scattered ulcerative and vesiculopustular lesions on the soft palate, tonsillar pillars, and tongue base, accompanied by a small, crusted lesion on the upper cutaneous lip. Figure 3: Cutaneous lesions Representative clinical photographs of cutaneous lesions obtained on hospital day 9, following laboratory confirmation of Mpox and after dedicated written informed consent for clinical photography and publication had been obtained from the patient. The images depict Mpox lesions in pustular and early crusting stages, consistent with the natural evolution of lesions approximately one week after their initial appearance. Panel A: dorsum of the hand with umbilicated pustular lesions; Panel B: palmar surface with firm pearly pustules, a recognised feature of Mpox; Panel C: trunk and lower abdomen showing scattered lesions in pustular and crusting stages, illustrating the polymorphic appearance characteristic of the current outbreak phenotype. All images were captured on the same day of illness (hospital day 9). A formal written patient narrative could not be obtained because the patient had been transferred to a designated isolation facility and was no longer available for an in-person interview. During the documented post-discharge telephone follow-up, the patient reported feeling well, with complete healing of lesions, no new lesions, and no recurrent symptoms. The patient’s clinical course is summarized in Table 1.
