B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

猴痘 暴发 爆炸物在马达加斯加造成 3,000 例病例

Explosive mpox outbreak causes 3,000 cases in Madagascar
新闻简报马达加斯加正在经历一场爆炸性的 猴痘 暴发,截至本周已有 3,022 例病例和 20 人死亡。2024 年在刚果民主共和国出现的 Clade 1b 菌株已通过人员流动和商业网络从马哈赞加传播到图阿马西纳和陶拉尼亚罗等沿海中心。专家警告说,可能存在漏报的情况,65% 的阳性率表明许多病例未被发现。高危人群包括性工作者、医护人员和弱势群体。马达加斯加卫生部正在与世卫组织合作,扩大检测和接触者追踪,尽管有限的资源和基础设施构成了挑战。与 DRC 毒株相比,暴发 的真实规模和严重程度仍不清楚。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-28。事件时间范围:截至本周,病例自2026年初开始记录。
地点与对象
国家: 马达加斯加.受影响地区:马哈赞加(最初的港口城市)、图阿马西纳和陶拉尼亚罗(繁忙的沿海枢纽)。受影响人群:普通人群,高危人群包括密集城市地区的性工作者、一线医护人员、幼儿、孕妇、免疫功能低下或艾滋病病毒感染者。
事件
已确认马达加斯加的 猴痘(进化枝 1b)爆炸物 暴发。该病毒通过人员流动和商业运输网络从马哈赞加传播到图阿马西纳和陶拉尼亚罗。暴发 与 2024 年在刚果民主共和国出现的 Clade 1b 菌株有关,该菌株促使 WHO 发布了全球公共卫生紧急状态声明。
规模、影响与风险
病例:截至本周已记录 3,022 例。死亡人数:20 人记录。传播:人员流动是主要驱动力,在没有事先流通的区域传播新的 暴发。风险:高阳性率(65%)表明漏报;真实病例数可能超过报告数字。严重的症状给弱势群体带来更大的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
回应:马达加斯加卫生部正在与世卫组织合作,扩大受影响最严重地区的检测能力和接触者追踪。正在规划 疫苗接种 活动,优先考虑高风险群体。不确定性:报告不足可能是由于资源有限、卫生优先事项相互竞争、误诊和文化耻辱造成的。目前尚不清楚循环菌株的严重程度是否与刚果民主共和国菌株不同。疫苗 的可用性和医疗基础设施在马达加斯加这个世界第五个最贫穷的国家受到限制。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Explosive mpox outbreak causes 3,000 cases in Madagascar

News Brief
Madagascar is experiencing an explosive mpox outbreak with 3,022 cases and 20 deaths recorded as of this week. The Clade 1b strain, which emerged in the DRC in 2024, has spread from Mahajanga to coastal hubs like Toamasina and Taolagnaro via human mobility and commercial networks. Experts warn that under-reporting is likely, with a 65% positivity rate suggesting many cases go undetected. High-risk groups include sex workers, healthcare workers, and vulnerable populations. Madagascar's Ministry of Health is collaborating with WHO to expand testing and contact tracing, though limited resources and infrastructure pose challenges. The outbreak's true scale and severity compared to the DRC strain remain unclear.
Original source text (English)
More than 3,000 cases of mpox have been recorded in Madagascar after the virus spread from a single port city to some of the country's busiest coastal hubs. A total of 3,022 cases and 20 deaths have been recorded as of this week, with cases in Toamasina and Taolagnaro accounting for more than half of all diagnoses. The outbreak has been linked to Clade 1b mpox, a strain that emerged in the Democratic Republic of the Congo in 2024, prompting the World Health Organization (WHO) to declare a global public health emergency at the time. "The main factor driving the continued spread of the disease is human mobility, seeding new outbreaks in areas where the virus has not circulated before," Professor Carlos Maluquer de Motes of the University of Surrey told The Telegraph. "Under-reporting is likely, with the real number of cases exceeding what is being reported." Madagascar is the world's fifth poorest country - Simon Townsley Mpox, formerly known as monkeypox , is a viral disease that causes fever, swollen lymph nodes and a distinctive lumpy rash . It was first identified in monkeys in 1958, with the first human case recorded in 1970 in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC). The virus is endemic in parts of Central and West Africa, where it typically spreads from infected animals to humans and then between people through close physical contact. Scientists believe the virus arrived in the Madagascan port city of Mahajanga via sea trading routes early this year, before spreading through the country's commercial transport networks to Toamasina and Taolagnaro. "These are two coastal cities with large commercial activity and consequently movement of people," Prof Maluquer de Motes said. "This facilitated the arrival of the virus and subsequent dissemination across highly dense urban transmission networks." Experts say the true scale of the outbreak may already be larger than official figures suggest. About 65 per cent of mpox tests carried out in Madagascar have come back positive, WHO data shows, which experts believe is a sign that many cases may be going undetected. "The high rate of cases testing positive for mpox indicates surveillance is inadequate," said Dr Jonas Albarnaz, a fellow at the Pirbright Institute. "Limited resources, competing public health priorities, misdiagnosis, and the cultural stigma surrounding mpox can contribute to under-reporting." Young children and those who are pregnant or immunocompromised, including those living with HIV, are at greater risk of developing severe symptoms . Prof Maluquer de Motes said vaccination campaigns should prioritise close contacts of infected people, frontline healthcare workers and other groups at highest risk of exposure. "Populations at the highest risk of infection are sex workers in highly dense urban areas like mining areas or commercial ports, and their contacts," he said. "Of course, frontline healthcare workers will also be at significant risk." Madagascar's Ministry of Health is working with the WHO to expand testing capacity and contact tracing in the worst-affected districts. It remains unclear whether the strain circulating in Madagascar differs in severity from the one that spread in the DRC. In Madagascar, the world's fifth poorest country, vaccine availability and healthcare infrastructure are likely to limit how far immunisation programmes can reach. 调查确认贝努埃社区水痘为 暴发 而非 猴痘 – 卫生专员 - Vanguard News

Investigation confirms Chickenpox outbreak not Mpox, in Benue community – Health Commissioner - Vanguard News
新闻简报贝努埃州卫生部在对社交媒体报道进行调查后确认,Korinya 社区最近发生的 暴发 是水痘,而不是 猴痘。两名成年居民出现发烧和皮疹,但临床评估显示典型的水痘症状,没有 猴痘 特有的特征,如手掌、脚底或口腔损伤。在发现与确诊病例没有流行病学联系后,该部启动了应急小组,并排除了 猴痘 的可能性。监测已在该地区加强,样品将在国家参考实验室进行测试。敦促居民保持冷静,避免传播未经证实的信息。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年7月28日。活动日期:2026年7月25日社交媒体报道;调查于2026年7月27日进行。
地点与对象
尼日利亚贝努埃州 Konshisha 地方政府区 Korinya 社区。受影响人口:两名成年居民。
事件
确认水痘 暴发,而非 猴痘。由贝努埃州卫生与公众服务部报告。临床评估后,调查驳回了 猴痘 的怀疑。
规模、影响与风险
病例:2 名成年居民出现发烧和水疱样皮疹。死亡人数:无报告。传播:与 猴痘 病例没有流行病学联系。风险:没有猴痘 暴发的证据;可能性被认为要低得多。
应对、状态与不确定性
立即启动调查;已部署公共卫生应急行动中心。监测 在 Korinya 和邻近社区加剧。样品将被送往国家参考实验室根据 NCDC 协议进行确认。未解决:没有报告新病例;正在进行中 监测。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Investigation confirms Chickenpox outbreak not Mpox, in Benue community – Health Commissioner - Vanguard News

News Brief
The Benue State Ministry of Health has confirmed that a recent outbreak in Korinya Community was Chickenpox, not Mpox, following an investigation into social media reports. Two adult residents developed fever and skin rashes, but clinical assessments showed typical Chickenpox symptoms with no Mpox-specific features like lesions on palms, soles, or mouth. The Ministry activated its emergency response team and ruled out Mpox after finding no epidemiological links to confirmed cases. Surveillance has been intensified in the area, with samples to be tested at the National Reference Laboratory. Residents were urged to remain calm and avoid spreading unverified information.
Original source text (English)
___ Vanguard News Home News Top Stories Politics Metro Business Sports Editorial Columns ALLURE E-editions Search for: Home News Top Stories Politics Metro Business Sports Editorial Columns ALLURE E-editions Home News Politics Metro Business Sports Editorial Columns Allure E-Editions Home » News » Investigation confirms Chickenpox outbreak not Mpox, in Benue community – Health Commissioner News Follow us on Google July 28, 2026 Investigation confirms Chickenpox outbreak not Mpox, in Benue community – Health Commissioner By Peter Duru, Makurdi The Benue State Ministry of Health and Human Services has dismissed reports of a suspected Mpox (Monkeypox) outbreak in Korinya Community, Konshisha Local Government Area, LGA, assuring residents that investigations showed the reported illness was consistent with Chickenpox (Varicella) and not Mpox. The Commissioner for Health and Human Services, Dr. Paul Ogwuche, who disclosed this in a statement he personally signed Monday, said the Ministry swung into action immediately after receiving social media reports on July 25 alleging that residents of Tyokula Achihi Residence, along the Vandeikya-Konshisha Road, had developed fever and widespread blister-like skin rashes suspected to be Mpox. According to him, “The Benue State Ministry of Health and Human Services wishes to reassure the good people of Benue State that the recent social media reports alleging an outbreak of Mpox in Korinya Community have been thoroughly investigated. Based on the findings available at this time, there is no evidence of an Mpox outbreak in Korinya Community, Konshisha LGA.” Dr. Ogwuche explained that the Ministry immediately activated its Public Health Emergency Operations Centre, deploying the Konshisha Local Government Disease Surveillance and Notification Officer, the State Disease Surveillance and Notification Officer, the State Epidemiologist and the World Health Organization (WHO) community surveillance network to verify the claims. He said the investigation identified two adult residents who had earlier developed fever followed by blister-like skin rashes but had already sought treatment at a private health facility and were recovering before the investigation team arrived. The Commissioner stated that a detailed clinical assessment revealed that the illness followed the typical course of Chickenpox, with the patients’ skin lesions already dried and crusted over. He added that there were no lesions on the palms, soles or mouth, which are key clinical features commonly associated with Mpox, while investigators also found no epidemiological link to any confirmed or suspected Mpox case. According to him, “The illness was clinically more consistent with Chickenpox than Mpox. The epidemiological investigation established no known contact with any confirmed or suspected Mpox case, making the likelihood of Mpox considerably lower.” Despite ruling out an Mpox outbreak, Dr. Ogwuche said surveillance had been intensified in Korinya and neighbouring communities, noting that any new suspected cases would have samples collected and sent to the National Reference Laboratory for confirmation in line with Nigeria Centre for Disease Control and Prevention (NCDC) protocols. He urged residents to remain calm and avoid spreading unverified information capable of causing unnecessary panic, while commending community members for promptly reporting the suspected illness. The Commissioner reaffirmed the commitment of the administration of Governor Hyacinth Alia to strengthening disease surveillance, emergency preparedness and rapid response systems, assuring that the Ministry would continue to investigate all suspected public health events using globally accepted scientific standards to safeguard the health of Benue residents. 贝努埃解雇了科尼亚的可疑 猴痘 - 尼日利亚太阳报

Benue dismisses suspected Mpox in Kornya - The Sun Nigeria
新闻简报贝努埃州政府驳斥了有关Konshisha LGA Korinya社区疑似猴痘 暴发的报道,调查证实这些病例在临床上与水痘相符。7月25日社交媒体报道后,该州启动了公共卫生应急行动中心并立即展开调查。由地方和州卫生官员以及世卫组织代表组成的联合小组对两名出现发烧和皮疹的成年居民进行了检查。临床评估,包括检查病变照片,证实该疾病遵循水痘的典型模式,没有 猴痘 特有的特征,如手掌/足底受累或口腔粘膜病变。两名患者均已在一家私人机构康复。该部强调不存在任何证据,但维持该地区加强的监测,并制定了如果出现新病例则对样本进行测试的协议。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年7月28日。活动日期:2026年7月25日(收到社交媒体报道时)。调查于2026年7月27日进行(发表声明)。
地点与对象
尼日利亚贝努埃州 Konshisha 地方政府区 Korinya 社区。受影响人口:两名成年居民。
事件
贝努埃州政府在调查证实这些病例在临床上与水痘相符后,驳回了疑似 猴痘 暴发 的报告。该事件被视为疑似公共卫生事件,促使公共卫生紧急行动中心 (PHEOC)、州 流行病学 单位以及疾病 流行病学 和通知系统启动。
规模、影响与风险
两名成年居民出现发烧和水疱样皮疹。两名患者均在私人医疗机构寻求 治疗 治疗,并显示出显着康复。该部表示,没有证据表明 Korinya 社区存在 猴痘 暴发。监测 在 Korinya 及周边社区得到加强。
应对、状态与不确定性
当地和州疾病 监测 官员、州流行病学家和世卫组织社区 监测 网络参与了联合调查。临床评估和照片审查证实该疾病与水痘相符,而不是 猴痘。未发现与确诊或疑似 猴痘 病例有已知接触。监测 仍然活跃,并制定了在出现新疑似病例时收集和测试样本的协议。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Benue dismisses suspected Mpox in Kornya - The Sun Nigeria

News Brief
Benue State Government has refuted reports of a suspected Mpox outbreak in Korinya Community, Konshisha LGA, after investigations confirmed the cases were clinically consistent with Chickenpox. Following social media reports on July 25, the state activated its Public Health Emergency Operations Centre and launched an immediate investigation. A joint team involving local and state health officials, along with WHO representatives, examined two adult residents with fever and skin rashes. Clinical assessment, including review of lesion photographs, confirmed the illness followed the typical pattern of Chickenpox, with no Mpox-specific features like palm/sole involvement or oral mucous membrane lesions. Both patients were already recovering at a private facility. The ministry emphasized no Mpox outbreak evidence exists but maintained intensified surveillance in the area, with protocols to test samples if new cases arise.
Original source text (English)
Sunday, August 2, 2026 Follow Us on Google Search Home National Columns Business China News Politics & Power Opinion Entertainment The Sun TV Sporting Sun The Sun Foundation Benue dismisses suspected Mpox in Kornya 28 July 2026 4:12 am WAT By Scholastica Hir Follow Us on Google From Scholastica Hir, Makurdi Benue State Government has dismissed reports of a suspected Mpox outbreak in Korinya Community, Konshisha Local Government Area, saying investigations confirmed the reported cases are clinically consistent with Chickenpox and not Mpox. The Commissioner for Health and Human Services, Dr. Paul Ogwuche, in a statement yesterday in Makurdi, said it launched an immediate investigation after receiving social media reports on July 25, alleging that several residents of Tyokula Achihi along the Vandeikya-Konshisha Road had developed severe fever and blister-like skin rashes suspected to be Mpox. Ogwuche said the report, which generated widespread concern, was treated as a suspected public health event, prompting the activation of the state’s Public Health Emergency Operations Centre (PHEOC), the State Epidemiology Unit and the Disease Surveillance and Notification System. According to him, a joint investigation involving the Konshisha Local Government Disease Surveillance and Notification Officer, the State Disease Surveillance and Notification Officer, the State Epidemiologist and the World Health Organisation (WHO) community surveillance network identified two adult residents who had experienced fever followed by widespread blister-like rashes. He explained that both patients had already sought treatment at a private health facility in Korinya and were showing significant recovery before the investigation team arrived. The ministry said detailed clinical assessment revealed that the illness followed the typical pattern of Chickenpox rather than Mpox. Other News Govt dispels rumours of Mpox outbreak in Benue He noted that the skin lesions had already dried and crusted over, indicating a self-limiting viral infection, while there was no involvement of the palms, soles or oral mucous membranes, features commonly associated with Mpox. He also explained that the investigation also established that neither patient had any known contact with confirmed or suspected Mpox cases, further reducing the likelihood of the disease. He said photographs of the skin lesions and accompanying clinical information reviewed by investigators also supported the conclusion that the illness was clinically compatible with Chickenpox. He said: “Based on the findings available at this time, there is no evidence of an Mpox outbreak in Korinya community in Konshisha LGA.” Despite the findings, the ministry said surveillance had been intensified in Korinya and surrounding communities to ensure that any emerging public health threat is detected promptly. It added that if new suspected cases emerge, clinical samples would be collected and sent to the National Reference Laboratory for laboratory confirmation, in line with Nigeria Centre for Disease Control and Prevention (NCDC) surveillance protocols. Response, status and uncertainty
A joint investigation involving local and state disease surveillance officers, the State Epidemiologist, and WHO community surveillance network was conducted. Clinical assessment and review of photographs confirmed the illness was compatible with Chickenpox, not Mpox. No known contact with confirmed or suspected Mpox cases was established. Surveillance remains active, with protocols in place to collect and test samples if new suspected cases emerge.
原文 / Source

B routine priorityPublic-health news

No Confirmed Mpox Case in Benue as Health Ministry Begins Investigation - thenewsnigeria.com.ng

News Brief
The Benue State Ministry of Health is investigating suspected Mpox cases reported on social media from Korinya, Konshisha Local Government Area. While no outbreak has been confirmed, the ministry has activated its surveillance system and deployed a team for on-site assessment and laboratory testing. Authorities emphasize that skin rashes and fever can result from various conditions, requiring laboratory confirmation for Mpox diagnosis. The public is urged to remain calm, avoid spreading unverified information, and report symptoms to healthcare facilities. The ministry continues to collaborate with national and international health agencies and will update the public as investigation results become available.
Original source text (English)

Explore The News magazine → The Benue State Ministry of Health and Human Services has become aware of reports circulating on social media about a suspected Mpox outbreak at the Tyokula Achihi Residence in Korinya, Konshisha Local Government Area. As stated in a statement signed by Dr. Paul Ejeh Ogwuche, Commissioner for Health and Human Services, Benue State, immediately upon the information coming to our attention, the Ministry activated its surveillance system in line with established public health protocols. The Director of Public Health was directed to liaise with the State Epidemiologist, who in turn immediately deployed the Disease Surveillance and Notification Officer (DSNO) for Konshisha Local Government Area to the reported location for on-the-ground assessment, case investigation and collection of appropriate clinical samples for laboratory testing. At this stage, no Mpox outbreak has been confirmed in Benue State. It is important to emphasise that skin rashes and fever can result from several medical conditions, and laboratory confirmation is required before any diagnosis of Mpox can be made. Discover more Health History Skin Conditions The Government of His Excellency, Rev. Fr. Dr. Hyacinth Iormem Alia, places the highest priority on protecting the health of every Benue citizen. Our disease surveillance system remains active across all 23 Local Government Areas, and we maintain close collaboration with the Nigeria Centre for Disease Control and Prevention (NCDC), the Federal Ministry of Health, the World Health Organization (WHO), and other development partners to ensure prompt detection and response to any public health event. The Ministry therefore appeals to members of the public to remain calm and avoid spreading unverified information capable of creating unnecessary panic. We encourage anyone experiencing fever accompanied by unexplained rashes or blisters to report immediately to the nearest health facility for proper medical evaluation and to avoid close physical contact until assessed by healthcare professionals. Discover more Medical Literature & Resources OBGYN Government We commend members of the community for promptly reporting the situation, as early reporting is essential for effective disease surveillance and response. The Ministry will continue to keep the public informed as soon as the investigation and laboratory findings become available. Should the results confirm Mpox or any other disease of public health importance, appropriate response measures will be implemented immediately in accordance with national guidelines. The Benue State Ministry of Health and Human Services remains committed to safeguarding the health and wellbeing of all residents of the state.
